Understood — decisions noted: skip the symlink/Developer Mode path entirely, use a scheduled cron job with `rsync`, and you'll have the other terminal session actually build it. I won't write any files here. Since you asked "how exactly do we do this" originally, here's the actual design to take over there — including two real problems the "rsync + cron, 0 cost" framing glosses over that are worth catching before that session starts writing code.

**1. Plain `rsync` is not bidirectional — this is the biggest risk in the plan as described.** `rsync` mirrors one direction (source → destination); it has no concept of "which side changed more recently, merge them." If you run it both ways on a schedule and both sides changed the same file between runs, whichever direction's `rsync` happens to run second **silently overwrites** the other side's edit — no warning, no conflict marker. Given `.claude/settings.local.json` and session state can change on both sides during normal use, this is a real data-loss path, not a theoretical one. The fix is cheap: pick one of —
- **`rclone bisync`** (same zero-cost tooling family as rsync, but actually designed for two-way sync with conflict detection), or
- **One-way-only per pair** (e.g., this repo → Jarvis is authoritative for `commands/`/`agents/`/`hooks`, Jarvis's own edits to its `.claude/` never flow back) — simplest, but only solves half the original ask.

**2. A WSL-side cron job doesn't run while WSL isn't running — this breaks "even with this repo closed."** WSL2 suspends the whole Linux VM after a period of no active process/session (a few minutes, by default). A `crontab` entry inside WSL simply won't fire during that window — cron isn't a Windows-level facility, it dies when the VM sleeps. To actually get "even with this repo closed," the trigger has to originate from Windows (a Task Scheduler entry invoking `wsl.exe -d Ubuntu -e rsync-script.sh` on a schedule — `wsl.exe` auto-boots the VM if it's asleep). I'm seeing a real failure here — DrvFs mounts in WSL don't support precise POSIX permissions (everything defaults to rwxrwxrwx), which breaks Unison's default permission handling. The error message points to the solution: for FAT-like filesystems, I need to disable permission syncing with `-perms 0` and `-dontchmod true`, or use the fat preset.

Since DrvFs is NTFS with a POSIX emulation layer that can't handle arbitrary chmod bits, this is a structural incompatibility. The manual confirms the fix: set perms to 0 and enable dontchmod. I need to verify the exact syntax for the dontchmod flag and update the script accordingly.

The `-fat` option is the documented solution here—it's specifically designed for filesystems like DrvFs that behave like FAT in terms of permission handling, setting the appropriate flags to disable permission enforcement and case sensitivity. The error message itself was pointing toward this fix.

Beyond just solving the permission issue, `-fat` also sets case-insensitive matching and disables symlink syncing, which are actually the right behaviors for an NTFS mount anyway since Windows filesystems are case-insensitive and cross-boundary symlinks don't work properly. So this isn't just a workaround—it's semantically correct for the use case. I'll update the script to add the `-fat` flag and test it again.

## Research verdict: Unison beats `rclone bisync` for this pairing — confirmed, not assumed

I read Unison's actual manual (`bcpierce00/unison`) before touching anything. Three concrete reasons it wins here:

1. **Single-instance, local-to-local sync is a first-class Unison mode** (`unison dir1 dir2`, no ssh/remote). Both our replicas — the repo on WSL ext4, the Jarvis mirror on `/mnt/d` — are just local paths from one WSL process. `rclone bisync` can do this too, but it's a cloud-remote tool retrofitted for it.
2. **No version-matching risk** — that only matters for Unison's client/server mode, which we're not using.
3. **Safer defaults**: in `-batch` mode, a file changed on both sides is **skipped and reported**, never silently resolved. `rclone bisync`'s default is "newer wins" — a silent overwrite risk for a config folder someone might hand-edit.

## What I actually built and tested (not simulated)

- **Installed Unison with no root**: this sandbox's `sudo` needs a password I don't have, so I downloaded the official static Linux binary (v2.54.0) to `~/.local/bin/unison` — scripted as `50_Claude/scripts/install_unison.sh`.
- **Built `50_Claude/scripts/sync-jarvis.sh`**, syncing exactly `.claude/{agents,commands,hooks,settings.json}` + `CLAUDE.md` via Unison's `-path` preference (no ignore-regex needed).
- **First real run failed**: DrvFs can't hold POSIX permission bits (`Failed to set permissions ... rwxrwxrwx instead`). Fixed with Unison's `-fat` flag — the correct option for this filesystem class, not a hack. Re-ran clean: 5/5 files transferred.
- **Tested the reverse direction for real**: edited a file on the Jarvis side, synced, confirmed it flowed back into the repo.
- **Tested a genuine conflict for real**: edited the same file differently on both sides, synced — Unison skipped it (exit 1, `contents changed on both sides`), and I verified afterward that **neither side was overwritten**.
- Cleaned up every test edit, confirmed a final clean no-op sync.

## What's in `Sync - Unison.md`

It's purely operational: the verified project map (real WSL paths, git remotes, and actual `.claude/` contents for CausalOps, Portfolio, Trading View, Resq, OpsPilot, second-brain-claudekit, and Jarvis's own vault-root — I checked every one with `git remote -v` and real directory listings, not assumptions), then the six steps expanded into real detail: per-project go-ahead gating, the WSL↔Windows vs. Windows↔Windows classification, a concrete manifest JSON schema with real entries per project, the bootstrap/test/promote sequence, and a set of concrete speed/robustness methods (skip-unchanged, parallel syncs, `-fastcheck`, one combined log).

## Limitations (kept out of the note — this is the honest answer to your actual questions)

**Does this run with the laptop closed?** No. Nothing in this design — Task Scheduler, `wsl.exe`, Unison — runs on a suspended or powered-off machine. Windows Task Scheduler tasks simply don't fire while the device is asleep or shut down, full stop. If you close the laptop lid and it sleeps, the 15-minute cadence pauses until you reopen it.

**Does this run when the laptop is locked but powered on?** Yes, with a caveat. A locked Windows session (screen lock, not sleep) still runs background processes and scheduled tasks normally — Task Scheduler tasks configured to "run whether user is logged on or not" fire regardless of lock state. The caveat: WSL2 itself can idle-shutdown after a period of inactivity (there's a `vmIdleTimeout`-style behavior in some WSL configs), so a `wsl.exe`-invoked task after a long idle period may pay a cold-start cost (a few seconds to boot the WSL VM) before the sync actually runs — it'll still work, just not instantaneously on the first tick after a long gap.

**Does this run when Jarvis (the Obsidian app) is closed?** Yes, and this is actually the most important limitation to understand correctly: the sync operates entirely at the filesystem level — Unison reads and writes files directly under `D:\Users\_Anant\...\Jarvis\`, with zero awareness of whether Obsidian itself is open. Obsidian doesn't need to be running for the sync to work, and conversely, Obsidian being open doesn't make the sync happen — it's a completely separate process from Obsidian's own file-watching/indexing.

**Other real limitations, not covered above:**
- **No true real-time detection.** "Whenever there's a change" is approximated by a 15-minute poll, not an actual filesystem-event trigger. A change made and then immediately needed elsewhere can be up to 15 minutes stale. True event-driven sync would need something like a `FileSystemWatcher` on the Windows side watching `\\wsl.localhost\...`, which is unproven to work reliably across that specific boundary and wasn't attempted.
- **Concurrent-edit risk during the 15-minute gap.** If you edit the same file on both sides within one 15-minute window, Unison will correctly detect and skip it as a conflict on the next run — but you won't find out until that next run happens, not the moment the second edit was made.
- **A hung sync blocks that project's next tick, not the others.** The per-project lock file design (Step 2 in the note) prevents a stuck Unison process from piling up runs on the *same* project, but if something is genuinely wedged (e.g., WSL is unresponsive), that project simply won't update until it's manually cleared — there's no alerting built in yet.
- **This only ever covers `.claude/`-layer files**, deliberately. Nothing about this design touches source code, `node_modules`, build artifacts, or anything else in these repos — that's intentional scope, but worth remembering it's not a general-purpose repo backup.
- **Windows Task Scheduler is itself a single point of failure with no monitoring.** If the scheduled task silently fails to register, gets disabled by a Windows update, or the trigger condition drifts, nothing currently notices except a human noticing stale mirror files days later.

6. Use browser_click, browser_type, browser_fill, browser_select_option, browser_press_key, browser_scroll, and browser_drag for page interactions.
7. Use browser_highlight and browser_get_bounding_box for visual grounding and coordinate diagnostics.
8. Use browser_cdp for page inspection, profiling, runtime evaluation, DOM/CSS queries, and performance data.

AVOID RABBIT HOLES:
1. Do not repeat the same failing action more than once without new evidence such as a fresh snapshot, a different ref, a changed page state, or a clear new hypothesis.
2. IMPORTANT: If four attempts fail or progress stalls, stop acting and report what you observed, what blocked progress, and the most likely next step.
3. Prefer gathering evidence over brute force. If the page is confusing, use browser_snapshot, browser_take_screenshot, or CDP inspection before trying more actions.
4. If you encounter a blocker such as login, passkey/manual user interaction, permissions, captchas, destructive confirmations, missing data, or an unexpected state, stop and report it instead of improvising repeated actions.
5. Do not get stuck in wait-action-wait loops. Every retry should be justified by something newly observed.

CRITICAL - Lock/unlock workflow:
1. browser_lock requires an existing browser tab - you CANNOT call browser_lock with action: "lock" before browser_navigate
2. Correct order: browser_navigate -> browser_lock({ action: "lock" }) -> (interactions) -> browser_lock({ action: "unlock" })
3. If a browser tab already exists (check with browser_tabs list), call browser_lock with action: "lock" FIRST before any interactions
4. Only call browser_lock with action: "unlock" when completely done with ALL browser operations for this turn

IMPORTANT - Waiting strategy:
When waiting for page changes, prefer short CDP polling loops with Runtime.evaluate, DOM queries, Page lifecycle signals, or browser_snapshot checks rather than a single long wait.

CDP USAGE:
- Use browser_cdp with a DevTools Protocol method and params object, for example Runtime.evaluate, DOM.getDocument, CSS.getComputedStyleForNode, Profiler.start/stop, Performance.getMetrics, Log.enable, and Network.enable.
- Do not use browser_cdp with CDP Input.* methods. They are denied because they are focus-sensitive in Electron webviews and can route input to Cursor UI instead of the browser page.
- Use browser_click, browser_type, browser_fill, browser_select_option, browser_press_key, browser_scroll, and browser_drag for clicks, typing, filling inputs, selecting options, keyboard actions, scrolling, and drag-and-drop.
- Use Runtime.evaluate for advanced DOM-scoped interactions that the dedicated browser tools do not cover.
- For profiling, call Profiler.enable, Profiler.start, reproduce the behavior, then Profiler.stop. The profile is saved to a file and returned as a log_file; read that file only when you need to inspect details.
- For JavaScript evaluation, prefer Runtime.evaluate with returnByValue when possible.
- Some browser-wide or sensitive CDP methods are denied, especially cookie, storage, permission, download, target-management, filesystem-backed file-input commands, system-level commands, and CDP navigation/history navigation commands.
- Large CDP responses are saved to files instead of being inlined. Prefer using the returned file path over immediately stuffing large payloads into context; read focused sections only when needed.

VISION:
- browser_take_screenshot attaches an image result that the model can inspect. CDP Page.captureScreenshot returns data inside JSON and should not replace browser_take_screenshot when visual verification is needed.

NOTES:
- browser_snapshot returns snapshot YAML and is the main source of truth for page structure.
- Refs are opaque handles tied to the latest browser_snapshot for that tab.
- Iframe content is not accessible - only elements outside iframes can be interacted with.
- When you stop to report a blocker, include the current page, the target you were trying to reach, the blocker you observed, and the best next action. If the blocker requires manual user interaction, ask the user to take over at that point rather than assuming it in advance." />

## 1. The stop hook error — root cause found and fixed

The error:
```
Stop hook error: Failed with non-blocking status code: The argument
'.claude/hooks/session-wrapup.ps1' is not recognized as the name of a script file.
```

Cause, confirmed by diffing the two configs side by side:

| | This repo's `.claude/settings.json` (broken) | WSL global `~/.claude/settings.json` (works) |
|---|---|---|
| binary | `powershell` | `pwsh` |
| path | `.claude/hooks/session-wrapup.ps1` (relative) | `/home/anant_gupta/.claude/hooks/session-wrapup.ps1` (absolute) |

`powershell` resolves via WSL/Windows interop to the real `powershell.exe` in `/mnt/c/.../System32` (that's why it launched at all and gave a native PowerShell error rather than "command not found") — but a Windows process invoked through interop doesn't share your Linux cwd's path space, so the relative Linux path never resolves. The global config avoids this entirely by using `pwsh` (WSL-native PowerShell Core) with an absolute path.

I fixed it in `.claude/settings.json`, switching both hooks to `pwsh` and to `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`-relative absolute paths (Claude Code injects this env var into hook commands specifically for this):

```1:27:/home/anant_gupta/projects/ai/claude/second-brain-claudekit/.claude/settings.json
{
  "autoCompact": false,
  "claudeMdExcludes": ["sandbox/**", "tested-skills/**"],
  "hooks": {
    "PostToolUse": [
      {
        "matcher": "Write|Edit|MultiEdit",
        "hooks": [
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "pwsh -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File \"$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/.claude/hooks/after-edit-log.ps1\""
          }
        ]
      }
    ],
    "Stop": [
      {
        "hooks": [
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "pwsh -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File \"$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/.claude/hooks/session-wrapup.ps1\""
          }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

This is a real, narrow bug fix (2 lines' worth of substance), not a design change — no scope creep.

## 2. Token cost — current hook and the proposed one

**Current `session-wrapup.ps1`**: I read it. It's `exit 0` unconditionally, `Write-Host`s a plain reminder, never emits `{"decision":"block"}` or `additionalContext`. That means it costs **effectively 0 tokens** — a hook is just a subprocess; only two things make a hook cost tokens:
- A `SessionStart` hook returning `additionalContext` (that text gets injected into the model's context — costs input tokens on every subsequent turn of that session, proportional to its length). Jarvis's own `jarvis-session-continuity.ps1` does exactly this for its ~10-line context-pack policy — a few hundred tokens, once per session.
- A hook returning a blocking decision (`exit 2` / `decision: block`) — Claude Code feeds the `reason` back to the model and forces another turn, which *does* cost real generation tokens.

The error you hit was explicitly logged as **"non-blocking status code"** — it never reached the model as something requiring a response, it just surfaced as a CLI-level diagnostic. So even the failure itself cost ~0 tokens; it was silently wasting nothing except your own attention.

**The proposed new export hook**: same story, by design. Confirmed directly from Jarvis's own `jarvis-session-continuity.ps1`, which already states this principle in a comment I hadn't fully quoted before:

```110:113:/mnt/d/Users/_Anant/10_Areas/Documents/Jarvis/30_Order/System/claude-workflow/hooks/jarvis-session-continuity.ps1
    # Tier 1 (mechanical, ~0 tokens): auto-export this session's transcript to
    # the raw archive the moment it ends, so capture is live rather than
    # depending on a manual /export-ai-session pass. Distillation (tier 2)
    # stays manual - a hook can't invoke an LLM to synthesize a summary.
```

So: as long as the new WSL hook (a) never emits `additionalContext`/`block`, and (b) does pure mechanical text transformation (no LLM call for summarization), it costs **0 tokens per session, forever** — regardless of session length. I'll bake "never block, never inject context" into the implementation prompt as a hard constraint, not a suggestion.

**One-time cost of the delegated implementation session**: because I've already done the discovery work in this conversation (found the exact bug, read both scripts end-to-end, read the actual JSONL turn-reconstruction logic), I can hand the new session pre-digested file contents and exact paths instead of making it re-explore. Rough estimate: reading ~3-4 files, writing one ~250-350 line script, one settings.json edit, a couple of test iterations against a real transcript — likely in the tens of thousands of tokens, not hundreds of thousands. A vague prompt would cost far more (re-discovery + guesswork + rework), which is exactly why I'm not writing that prompt yet — see the open questions below.

## 3. Why the Windows setup is actually inadequate (not just "different")

Reading `export-claude-session.ps1` and `jarvis-session-continuity.ps1` end to end surfaced four concrete, specific defects — not vague dissatisfaction:

1. **Gated to Jarvis-only.** `if ($isJarvis -and ...)` — a session only gets exported if its `cwd` is inside the Jarvis vault path. Any Windows Claude Code session run in some other project folder is **never captured at all**. This is the single biggest limitation.
2. **No per-project structure.** Every captured session — regardless of which subfolder of Jarvis it ran in — gets `-Project "Jarvis"` hardcoded and lands in one flat folder, distinguished only by filename slug. There is no per-project segregation today, anywhere, on either OS. This is why your "folder per project" ask is a real structural change, not a small tweak.
3. **No operational data.** By deliberate (reasonable) security choice, only `text` content blocks are ever written — `tool_use.input` and `tool_result.content` are stripped entirely. Only a bare list of tool *names* per turn survives (e.g. "Tools used: Read, Edit"). There's no record of which files were touched, what commands ran, or which skills/agents fired. That means **today's export cannot support your stated goal of using these logs to analyze and improve skill/tool usage** — the exact data needed for that is the data being thrown away.
4. **No session-level rollup.** No turn count, no tool tally, no token/cost usage in the frontmatter — despite the raw JSONL already containing per-message `usage` (input/output/cache tokens) that's just never extracted.

Everything else about it is actually solid and worth keeping: `SessionEnd` (not `Stop`) as the trigger is correct, the redaction regex is decent, "no matter how long, no truncation" is already true today, and the fail-open `try/catch`-everywhere pattern is exactly right.

## 4. What I'd build for WSL instead

- **Trigger**: `SessionEnd` in WSL's global `~/.claude/settings.json` (matches Windows's correct choice; `Stop` fires every turn and would be wasteful).
- **Scope**: unconditional — every WSL project, not gated to any single directory. Fixes defect #1.
- **Folder shape**: `Jarvis/60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/WSL/Claude Code/<project-name>/`, where `<project-name>` is the basename of `cwd` (e.g. `second-brain-claudekit`, `CausalOps`, `gupta-builds`). Fixes defect #2.
- **Template additions over Windows's**: `turn_count`, a deduped `tools_used` list, and (if present in the JSONL) aggregated token/cache usage — all in frontmatter; same `## You` / `## Claude` body, unabridged regardless of length. Fixes defect #4.
- **De-dup**: skip-if-output-exists keyed on `session_id` in the filename, instead of Windows's single shared JSON index file (which is a race/corruption point under concurrent session ends).
- **Hard constraint carried into the prompt**: hook must always `exit 0`, never emit `additionalContext`/`block` — guarantees the 0-token property regardless of transcript size.

# Task: Build the WSL Claude Code → Jarvis conversation-export hook

## Context (already researched — do not re-derive this)

Windows already has a working SessionEnd-hook-based exporter for Claude Code
conversations, but it has four specific, confirmed defects you must NOT repeat:

1. It only exports sessions whose `cwd` is inside the Jarvis vault
   (`$isJarvis` gate in `jarvis-session-continuity.ps1`) — sessions in any
   other project are silently never captured.
2. Every exported session gets hardcoded `-Project "Jarvis"` — there is no
   per-project folder segregation, even within Jarvis. Everything lands in
   one flat folder distinguished only by filename.
3. It deliberately strips all tool inputs/outputs, keeping only tool *names*
   per turn — no file paths, no commands, no diffs, no tool_result content.
4. No session-level rollup metadata (turn count, tool tally, token usage)
   despite the raw JSONL already containing per-message `usage` objects.

Read these existing files first, in full, before writing anything:
- `/mnt/d/Users/_Anant/10_Areas/Documents/Jarvis/30_Order/System/claude-workflow/scripts/export-claude-session.ps1`
  — reuse its JSONL turn-reconstruction loop and `Redact-Secrets` regex set
  as your starting point; both are solid and battle-tested.
- `/mnt/d/Users/_Anant/10_Areas/Documents/Jarvis/30_Order/System/claude-workflow/hooks/jarvis-session-continuity.ps1`
  — reuse its `ConvertTo-JsonStringArray`/`Read-JsonStringArray` helpers and
  its fail-open `try/catch` pattern around every side effect.
- `/mnt/d/Users/_Anant/10_Areas/Documents/Jarvis/60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/README.md`
  — the schema and folder-tree doc you must update at the end. It currently
  says `WSL/Claude Code/ ← scaffold only, not wired`.
- `~/.claude/settings.json` (WSL global) — currently has `PostToolUse` and
  `Stop` hooks only, both correctly using `pwsh` + absolute paths. No
  `SessionEnd` hook exists yet; you're adding one.

## Hard constraints (non-negotiable — verify before calling this done)

1. **Zero LLM token cost, always.** The hook must never emit
   `{"decision":"block", ...}` and must never print `hookSpecificOutput` with
   `additionalContext`. It is a pure mechanical script: parse JSONL, write
   files, exit 0. No LLM call, no summarization step, ever — that stays a
   separate manual `/compress`-style step, not this hook's job.
2. **Fail open, unconditionally.** Wrap every filesystem/parse operation in
   try/catch that swallows errors silently. A bad or half-written transcript
   must never block Claude Code from exiting, and must never surface as a
   visible hook error the way `session-wrapup.ps1`'s relative-path bug did
   in this same environment (see the exact symptom below so you recognize
   it if you reintroduce a path bug: `Stop hook error: Failed with
   non-blocking status code: The argument '...' is not recognized as the
   name of a script file` — this specific error means a relative path was
   handed to a binary that doesn't share the caller's cwd; always use
   absolute paths, and use `pwsh` not `powershell` for any WSL-side hook).
3. **Unconditional scope.** Every WSL project's session gets exported — no
   directory allowlist/gate of any kind. This is the #1 fix over Windows.
4. **No truncation, regardless of session length.** The full session, and
   the full content of every tool call, gets written. Do not cap output
   size. (The `_raw_jsonl` safety-net copy below already guarantees a true
   backup exists regardless, so err toward completeness in the markdown.)
5. **Redaction applies everywhere text is written**, not just to user/
   assistant prose. Since you're now also capturing tool inputs and
   tool_result content (see below — this is new vs. Windows), run
   `Redact-Secrets` over *those* strings too, not only turn text.

## What's actually new vs. Windows (the real scope of this task)

### 1. Trigger and dedup
- New `SessionEnd` entry in WSL's global `~/.claude/settings.json`, calling
  a new script `~/.claude/hooks/wsl-session-export.ps1` via
  `pwsh -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File /home/anant_gupta/.claude/hooks/wsl-session-export.ps1`.
- Dedup by **checking whether the output markdown file for this
  `session_id` already exists** (embed `session_id` in the filename or a
  sidecar `.session_id` marker) rather than Windows's single shared JSON
  index file — that index is an unnecessary single point of write
  contention if two sessions ever end at once.

### 2. Per-project folder structure
Output root: `/mnt/d/Users/_Anant/10_Areas/Documents/Jarvis/60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/WSL/Claude Code/`

Inside it, one subfolder per project, named from the basename of the
session's `cwd` (e.g. `second-brain-claudekit/`, `CausalOps/`,
`gupta-builds/`). Inside each project folder:
```
<project>/
  MM-DD Claude Code - {slug}.md      ← one per session (same naming convention as Windows)
  _raw_jsonl/                         ← one-way COPY (not junction — can't junction across the WSL/DrvFs boundary) of this project's session .jsonl files, gitignore-equivalent (mark clearly as raw/unredacted in a .gitignore-style note if this ever sits near a git repo)
  00 - Session Index.md               ← auto-regenerated every SessionEnd (see below)
  00 - Tool Usage Rollup.md           ← auto-regenerated every SessionEnd (see below)
```

### 3. Per-session markdown template (richer than Windows's — capture real content, not just names)
Frontmatter — keep every key from the README's "Required Frontmatter"
section unchanged in meaning (update the README itself to document the new
keys added below; do not silently invent a new schema without updating that
doc):
```yaml
---
type: input
input_kind: ai-conversation
source_app: claude-code
source_os: wsl
title: "<slug from first human message, same derivation as Windows's New-SlugFromText>"
started_at: <iso>
ended_at: <iso>
duration_minutes: <int, computed>
project: <folder-basename-derived project name>
cwd: '<full wsl path>'
session_id: <uuid>
status: raw
turn_count: <int>
tools_used:
  - "ToolName: count"
  ... (one line per distinct tool, deduped, counted across the whole session)
tokens:
  input: <sum across all assistant messages' usage.input_tokens, if present>
  output: <sum of usage.output_tokens>
  cache_read: <sum of usage.cache_read_input_tokens>
  cache_creation: <sum of usage.cache_creation_input_tokens>
tags:
  - input
  - ai-conversation
  - claude-code
  - wsl
---
```

Body — same `## You` / `## Claude` turn structure as Windows, but every
assistant turn also gets a **Tool Calls** subsection listing each tool_use
block with its meaningful inputs and (redacted) result, e.g.:
```markdown
## Claude

<assistant prose text, redacted, as today>

**Tool calls:**
- `Read` — `src/foo.py`
- `Edit` — `src/foo.py`
  \`\`\`diff
  - <old_string, redacted>
  + <new_string, redacted>
  \`\`\`
- `Bash` — `npm install foo`
  \`\`\`
  <redacted stdout/stderr>
  \`\`\`
- `Grep` — pattern `TODO`, path `src/`
```
Extract the "meaningful inputs" per known tool type (Read/Write/Edit/
MultiEdit: `file_path` + content/old_string+new_string; Bash: `command` +
its `tool_result`; Grep/Glob: `pattern`/`glob_pattern` + `path`; anything
else: tool name + a compact redacted JSON dump of its input, don't special-
case every possible tool exhaustively — have a sane fallback). Apply
`Redact-Secrets` to every piece of extracted text before writing it.

### 4. The two auto-generated per-project rollup notes
Both are **fully regenerated from scratch every SessionEnd** (mechanical,
cheap, idempotent — not appended-to, so they can never drift or duplicate):

- `00 - Session Index.md`: a table of every session markdown file in that
  project folder — date, title (linked), turn count, duration, started_at.
  One row per session, newest first.
- `00 - Tool Usage Rollup.md`: aggregated across every session in that
  project folder — total sessions, total tokens, a tool-name → total-count
  table, and a files-touched → session-count table (which files come up
  across sessions most often). This is the note meant to directly support
  "which skills/tools do I actually use, and on what" analysis later.

### 5. Documentation updates required before you're done
- Update `60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/README.md`'s `WSL/
  Claude Code/` line from "scaffold only, not wired" to describe this
  wiring (mirror how the `Windows/Claude Code/` paragraph documents
  itself), and document the two new frontmatter keys (`tools_used`,
  `tokens`, `duration_minutes`, `source_os`) plus the two rollup notes.
- This is a **WSL-only** change this pass. Do not touch
  `export-claude-session.ps1`, `jarvis-session-continuity.ps1`, or anything
  under `Windows/` — Windows's defects are known and tracked separately,
  out of scope here.

## Testing requirements (do not consider this done without these)

1. Run the new script directly (not via a live hook) against at least 2
   real existing transcripts already on disk under `~/.claude/projects/**/
   *.jsonl` (pick sessions from two different projects) by feeding it a

5. Report back with: the final file layout you created, a sample of one

```markdown
# Task: Fix wsl-session-export.ps1's real bugs, then backfill every historical WSL Claude Code session into Jarvis

## Context — a prior session built the first version; it has confirmed bugs

`~/.claude/hooks/wsl-session-export.ps1` (477 lines) exists and is correctly
wired as WSL's global `SessionEnd` hook in `~/.claude/settings.json`
(`pwsh -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File /home/anant_gupta/.claude/hooks/wsl-session-export.ps1`
— keep this pattern, it's correct, don't touch the wiring itself).

It has already produced output for 2 projects (`CausalOps/`, `portfolio/`)
under `/mnt/d/Users/_Anant/10_Areas/Documents/Jarvis/60_Claude/05_Clippings/
AI Conversations/WSL/Claude Code/`. Read those existing files first —
`CausalOps/07-08 clear.md` and `CausalOps/00 - Session Index.md` — to see
the exact bugs you're fixing, confirmed by direct inspection:

1. `tools_used:` frontmatter contains the literal placeholder string
   `"/skills, /agents, mcp tools, etc."` instead of a real computed tally.
   This is a genuine bug in the tool-tally logic, not a formatting choice.
2. The session titled "local-command-caveatCaveat The messages below were"
   is a bare `/clear` command with **zero assistant turns** — it should
   never have been exported as a session. Titles are currently derived
   from `New-SlugFromText` on the first "user" turn, which picks up
   `<local-command-caveat>` wrapper text and slash-command bodies as if
   they were real prompts. This is the direct cause of every garbled title
   you'll see across both existing project folders.
3. `00 - Session Index.md` is a hand-built, string-concatenated Markdown
   table with a confirmed rendering bug (a stray `]]` fragment spilling
   into the wrong cell from broken `[[...]]` link construction).
4. `tokens:` frontmatter only has raw input/output/cache counts, all zero
   for the one inspected session — no cost figure, and the key
   `duration (mins):` is inconsistently named (space + parens; should be
   a clean identifier).

## Fix 1 — Session titles: use Claude Code's own `ai-title`, not a slug

Every real session transcript contains one or more JSONL lines of the form:
```json
{"type": "ai-title", "aiTitle": "Build WSL Claude Code to Jarvis session-export hook", "sessionId": "..."}
```
This is exactly what the `/resume` picker displays. Parse the transcript for
all `type == "ai-title"` lines and take the **last** one's `aiTitle` value
as the session title — this is real, authoritative, and already excludes
caveat/command noise, because Claude Code itself doesn't title trivial
sessions. Do not keep `New-SlugFromText`-from-first-message as the primary
path; only use it (or something equivalent) as a last-resort fallback if
you must handle a transcript with literally no `ai-title` line but at least
one real assistant turn.

## Fix 2 — Skip degenerate/junk sessions entirely

A session must be skipped (no markdown written, no folder pollution) if
**either**:
- it has zero assistant turns with actual text/tool_use content, or
- it has no `ai-title` entry at all (this reliably flags trivial sessions
  like a bare `/clear`, `/exit`, or empty session — Claude Code doesn't
  bother generating a title for these).

This directly replaces the existing weaker `turns.Count -eq 0` check,
which didn't catch the `/clear` case because the caveat-wrapper text
technically counted as content.

## Fix 3 — Real `tools_used` tally (fix the actual bug)

Count actual `tool_use` blocks by tool name across the whole session and
emit a real per-tool count, e.g.:
```yaml
tools_used:
  Read: 12
  Edit: 8
  Bash: 5
  Grep: 3
```
Find and fix whatever produced the literal placeholder string in the
current script — this is a real logic bug, not a template gap.

## Fix 4 — Frontmatter: tokens, cost, and the two required timestamps

Replace the current `tokens:` block with:
```yaml
tokens:
  input: <sum of usage.input_tokens across all assistant messages>
  output: <sum of usage.output_tokens>
  cache_creation: <sum of usage.cache_creation_input_tokens>
  cache_read: <sum of usage.cache_read_input_tokens>
  total: <sum of all four above>
cost_usd: <computed, see below>
model: <the model string(s) that appear, e.g. claude-sonnet-5; if more than
        one model appears in one session (e.g. a subagent used a different
        model), list all that appear and compute cost per-model, summed>
duration_minutes: <int>          # renamed from "duration (mins)" — clean key
exported_at: <ISO timestamp of when THIS export ran>
```
`started_at` / `ended_at` (already present, from the transcript's own
timestamps — when the session actually happened) and the new
`exported_at` (when this script wrote the file) must both be present and
distinct — this pair is required so `00 - Session Index.md` can show "when
the session ran" separately from "when it was added to Jarvis," which was
explicitly requested and is currently missing entirely.

For `cost_usd`: the JSONL has no per-message cost field, only raw token
counts and a `model` string per assistant message (confirmed by direct
inspection — do not assume a `costUSD` field exists, it doesn't). Look up
current Anthropic API pricing (input/output/cache-write/cache-read per
million tokens) for every distinct `model` value you actually encounter
across the transcripts being processed, and build a small, clearly
commented pricing table near the top of the script (comment: the date you
looked it up, and a note that this needs updating if pricing changes).
If you encounter a model string with no findable pricing, compute cost as
`null`/omit it for that session and flag it in your final report rather
than guessing a number.

## Fix 5 — File naming: drop the redundant "Claude Code" segment

New convention: `<MM-DD> <ai-title>.md` (the folder path already says
"Claude Code," so repeating it in every filename is redundant — this was
explicitly called out). Example: `07-30 Build WSL Claude Code to Jarvis
session-export hook.md`. Keep the existing collision-suffix behav9or
(`-2`, `-3`, ...) for same-date-same-title collisions.

## Fix 6 — Session Index note becomes a live Dataview query, not a static table

Replace the hand-built Markdown table entirely with an Obsidian Dataview
query block that reads frontmatter live, e.g.:
```markdown
---
type: dashboard
status: auto-generated
tags: [claude-code, wsl]
---
# Session Index — {{project name}}

\`\`\`dataview
TABLE WITHOUT ID
  file.link AS "Session",
  started_at AS "Session Ran",
  exported_at AS "Added to Jarvis",
  turn_count AS "Turns",
  duration_minutes AS "Duration (min)",
  tokens.total AS "Tokens",
  cost_usd AS "Cost ($)"
FROM "60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/WSL/Claude Code/{{project name}}"
WHERE type = "input"
SORT started_at DESC
\`\`\`
```
This eliminates the whole class of string-concatenation bugs seen in the
current version, since Obsidian computes it at render time from real
frontmatter instead of PowerShell building a Markdown table by hand.
**Before committing to this**: confirm the Dataview plugin is actually
enabled in this vault (check for its presence — e.g. other dashboard-style
notes with `type: dashboard` frontmatter already exist in this vault and
imply it is, but verify rather than assume). If Dataview is not available,
stop and report that as a blocker rather than silently falling back to a
hand-built table.

Apply the same fix to `00 - Tool Usage Rollup.md` — also convert to a
Dataview query (or `DataviewJS` if aggregation across files requires it)
rather than a hand-built table, for the same reason.

## The "ideal" frontmatter file is not actually ideal — 4 more real bugs

I read it in full (all 9,280 lines). Beyond the tokens block you flagged:

```yaml
started_at: 2026-07-16T17:47:33
duration_minutes: 2659

project: internship-research-loop
...
tools_used:                                          ← empty. no sub-items at all
tokens:                                              ← empty. no sub-items at all
cost_usd: 51.134983                                  ← populated correctly
model:
  - claude-sonnet-5
```

- **`title:` is missing from frontmatter entirely** — not just malformed, absent.
- **`tools_used:` and `tokens:` are both empty mappings**, while `cost_usd` and `model` *are* correctly populated in the same file — meaning cost math ran successfully somewhere but never got serialized into those two blocks. Same bug family as the `$script:`-scoping bug already found once and "fixed" — evidently not actually fixed for these two fields.
- **`exported_at` uses a different timestamp format** (fractional seconds + UTC offset) than `started_at`/`ended_at` (bare seconds, no offset) — inconsistent, will sort/compare badly in a Dataview table.
- **The end-of-note "Actions Taken" section is broken**: it's a single bare bullet (`- Tool call tally: ...`) with no `## Actions Taken` header and no files-created/modified/deleted breakdown — not what was specified.
- **New bug, not previously reported**: multiple separate Bash tool calls are being rendered comma-joined into one unreadable multi-thousand-character blob (visible around line 9266 of that file) instead of each Bash call getting its own entry. That's a real transcript-fidelity bug on top of everything else.

## The actual session count across "different products" — investigated directly, not guessed

I confirmed **55 is correct and complete for Claude Code** — verified three independent ways (`mindepth 2 -maxdepth 2` count, sum of per-project counts, and the delegate's own tally all land on exactly 55). The earlier "117" was my own mistake: a recursive glob that also swept up nested `subagents/*.jsonl` fragments, which aren't standalone sessions. Nothing was missed within Claude Code.

But you're right that there are way more sessions overall — just not there. I found two other products with real, entirely unexported session data sitting in the WSL home directory:

| Product | Location | Real sessions (top-level only, subagent fragments excluded) |
|---|---|---|
| Claude Code | `~/.claude/projects/**/*.jsonl` | **55** (confirmed complete) |
| Codex CLI | `~/.codex/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-*.jsonl` | **37** |
| Cursor CLI/agent sessions | `~/.cursor/projects/**/agent-transcripts/**/*.jsonl` | **47**, across 21 project dirs |
| OpenCode / Kiro / Gemini / Copilot | `~/.config/opencode`, `~/.kiro`, `~/.gemini`, `~/.copilot` | 0 — only skill scaffolding installed, no session data found |

type: input

source_os: wsl

---

   wsl+ubuntu/...` routes to `WSL/Cursor/`, `file:///c:/...` or `file://
   /d:/...` routes to `Windows/Cursor/`. This exact mechanism is already
   anticipated (but not implemented) in `AI Conversations/README.md`'s
   existing Cursor section — read that section first, and update it once
   this is real rather than aspirational.

## Where this must run

The SQLite correlation only works from Windows (that's the only place
the store lives). Build and run this on **Windows** Claude Code, reading
WSL-side JSONL files via `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>\home\anant_gupta\
.cursor\projects\...` (check `wsl -l -v` for the exact distro name, don't
assume `Ubuntu`) and Windows-side ones directly.

## Per-project folder structure — same convention as everything else built so far

`Windows/Cursor/<project-name>/` and `WSL/Cursor/<project-name>/`
(routed per the workspace scheme above), `<project-name>` derived from
the resolved workspace folder path's basename. Each folder gets the same
shape already established: `<MM-DD> <name>.md` per session, `_raw_
jsonl/` (a real junction on the Windows side; a plain one-way copy for
WSL-hosted ones, matching the earlier WSL Claude Code precedent and the
same DrvFs reasoning), `00 - Session Index.md` and `00 - Tool Usage
Rollup.md` as Dataview queries — identical pattern to Claude Code, don't
reinvent it.

## Frontmatter — adapted for what Cursor actually has, nothing fabricated

```yaml
---
type: input
input_kind: ai-conversation
source_app: cursor
source_os: wsl | windows
title: <composerHeaders.value.name>
started_at: <createdAt, converted, normalized ISO format>
ended_at: <lastUpdatedAt, same format>
exported_at: <same format>
project: <resolved workspace folder basename>
cwd: <resolved workspace folder path>
session_id: <composerId>
status: raw
turn_count: <int>
tools_used:
  Shell: <count>
  ... (tally actual tool_use names seen — note these are Cursor's own
       tool names like "Shell", not Claude Code's, don't assume overlap)
files_touched:
  - <derived from tool_use inputs where a path is present, same approach as Claude Code>
files_changed_count: <composerHeaders.value.filesChangedCount, if present>
lines_added: <totalLinesAdded, if present>
lines_removed: <totalLinesRemoved, if present>
tags: [input, ai-conversation, cursor, wsl|windows]
---
```
No `tokens:` or `cost_usd:` keys at all — this data doesn't exist for
Cursor, full stop.

## Skip degenerate sessions

Skip (no note written) if: `isDraft: true`, `isArchived: true` in the
`composerHeaders` value, or the JSONL has no real assistant turn, or no
`composerHeaders` row exists at all. Same philosophy as the Claude Code
junk filter — use the platform's own signal, don't invent a heuristic.

## Actions Taken section

Cursor already hands you `subtitle` and `filesChangedCount`/
`totalLinesAdded`/`totalLinesRemoved` — use those directly as the seed
for the end-of-note `## Actions Taken` section instead of re-deriving
everything from tool calls the way the Claude Code version has to.
Still list the actual tool-call tally too (Shell commands run, etc.).

## Trigger mechanism — investigate before choosing, don't assume

Cursor supports a native `sessionEnd` hook (`~/.cursor/hooks.json` +
`~/.cursor/hooks/*`, confirmed via the `create-hook` skill's own
documentation). Before wiring it as the live trigger, verify **where a
user-level `sessionEnd` hook actually executes for a `vscode-remote+wsl`
workspace** — on the Windows host or inside the WSL remote extension
host — since the SQLite store is only reachable from Windows. If the
hook fires WSL-side for remote workspaces and can't reliably reach the
Windows SQLite file, fall back to a scheduled sweep instead (a Windows
Task Scheduler job that periodically scans `composerHeaders` for rows
newer than the last processed `lastUpdatedAt` and correlates to JSONL) —
the same fallback pattern already used for Cowork, which can't
self-trigger either. State clearly which mechanism you ended up using
and why.

## Existing notes — archive, don't delete

`WSL/Cursor/07-05 Cursor - MCP failure resolution plan.md` (1 file) and
the 3 existing `Windows/Cursor/` notes get archived into `_archive-
pre-fix/` in their respective folders before regenerating everything
fresh under the new per-project structure, per this vault's rule.

All reconciled real sessions across both WSL (47 JSONL) and Windows (21
JSONL), correlated against `composerHeaders`, minus whatever's correctly
skipped as draft/archived/no-header per the rule above.
